# Third-party notices

Bag is free software, licensed under the GNU General Public License, version 3 only (GPL-3.0-only): see [LICENSE](LICENSE). Its source code is at https://github.com/UXAVIA/bag.

Bag is built with the third-party software below. This file lists it, says which terms each part is used under, and reproduces the licence notices. The app shows the same notices on its licence page, under Settings → About → Open-source licences.

It is generated by `tools/licenses/generate.sh` from what this tree pins: `pubspec.lock`, the `tor` plugin's `Cargo.lock` (UXAVIA/tor at `3c379bc176f1a2d50ac5229e25fa272514d34d31`), and the plugins' Gradle and CocoaPods files, with Flutter 3.47.9. Don't edit it by hand.

- [Dart packages](#dart-packages): 107, of which 6 only in the Google Play and App Store builds
- [Rust crates](#rust-crates): 469 crates compiled into Bag's Tor library, the 5 C libraries bundled in them, and the Rust standard library
- [Android libraries](#android-libraries): 141, of which 16 only in the Google Play build
- [iOS libraries](#ios-libraries): none beyond Flutter and Apple's system frameworks
- [Fonts](#fonts): Material Icons font, bundled by Flutter 3.47.9; Bag draws its sat symbol itself

## Dart packages

Every package the app depends on at run time (no development-only packages), with the licence file Flutter shows for it in the app. Federated plugins bring their implementations for every platform (`*_linux`, `*_windows`, `*_web` …); they are listed although the Android and iOS builds don't use them. Packages marked *store builds only* are the ones CI adds for Google Play and the App Store (in-app purchase and the rating prompt).

## Rust crates

The crates `cargo` compiles into Bag's Tor library (`librust_lib_tor`, the `tor` plugin's native half) for the four targets Bag ships: Android on arm64, armv7 and x86_64, and iOS on arm64. They come from the plugin's `Cargo.lock`, and include build-time crates and macros. Where a crate offers a choice (`A OR B`), the licence it is used under is named. For an MPL-2.0 crate, the source of its unmodified files is on crates.io, at the address given. A crate's licence text is the file in its package, or, when the package has none, its repository's at the commit it was published from; failing both, the standard text, said as such. Notices that come with a crate (NOTICE, COPYRIGHT) follow its licence.

##### R153

Used by: objc2-core-foundation 0.3.2 (notice), objc2-io-kit 0.3.2 (notice)

````text
# License

The licensing of these crates is a bit complicated:
- The crates `objc2`, `block2`, `objc2-foundation` and `objc2-encode` are
  [currently][#23] licensed under [the MIT license][MIT].
- All other crates are trio-licensed under the [Zlib], [Apache-2.0] or [MIT]
  license, at your option.

Furthermore, the crates are (usually automatically) derived from Apple SDKs,
and that may have implications for licensing, see below for details.

[#23]: https://github.com/madsmtm/objc2/issues/23
[MIT]: https://opensource.org/license/MIT
[Zlib]: https://zlib.net/zlib_license.html
[Apache-2.0]: https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0


## Apple SDKs

These crates are derived from Apple SDKs shipped with Xcode. You can obtain a
copy of the Xcode license at:

https://www.apple.com/legal/sla/docs/xcode.pdf

Or by typing `xcodebuild -license` in your terminal.

From reading the license, it is unclear whether distributing derived works
such as these crates are allowed?

But in any case, to practically use these crates, you will have to link, and
that only works when you have the correct Xcode SDK available to provide the
required `.tbd` files, which is why we choose to still use the normal SPDX
identifiers in the crates (Xcode is required to use the crates, and when using
Xcode you have already agreed to the Xcode license).
````

CharIndices implementation of the Rust standard library at revision
ab32548539ec38a939c1b58599249f3b54130026
(https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/blob/ab32548539ec38a939c1b58599249f3b54130026/library/core/src/str/iter.rs).

## Android libraries

The libraries the Android build packages, besides Flutter's engine (whose notices Flutter lists itself). The app, each plugin it uses (its `android/build.gradle`) and the Flutter Android embedding declare the libraries marked *Declared by*; the Kotlin Gradle plugin adds `kotlin-stdlib` to every Kotlin module. Their own runtime dependencies, marked *Required by*, come from the libraries' Maven POMs, at the highest version requested, as Gradle picks it. That is read from the published metadata, not from a Gradle build, so it can list a little more than the APK holds.

Libraries marked *Google Play build only* come with the in-app purchase and rating plugins CI adds to the Play build; the GitHub and F-Droid builds don't contain them. Some of them are proprietary Google libraries, distributed under Google's terms, which are linked rather than reproduced.

#### `androidx.activity:activity` 1.8.1

Apache-2.0 · text [Apache-2.0](#apache-20)

Required by: `androidx.activity:activity-ktx`, `androidx.biometric:biometric`, `androidx.fragment:fragment`

## iOS libraries

Bag's iOS plugins (their podspecs and Swift packages) depend only on Flutter and on Apple's system frameworks (StoreKit in the App Store build), so there are no further iOS libraries to list. The Rust crates above are linked into the iOS app too.

Checked: `flutter_foreground_task 8.17.0`, `flutter_local_notifications 19.5.0`, `flutter_secure_storage_darwin 0.2.0`, `home_widget 0.9.3`, `in_app_purchase_storekit 0.4.17`, `in_app_review 2.0.12`, `local_auth_darwin 1.6.0`, `path_provider_foundation 2.4.2`, `shared_preferences_foundation 2.5.4`, `tor 0.2.2`, `url_launcher_ios 6.3.4`, `workmanager 0.7.0`

## Fonts

Text uses the platform's fonts, and the sat symbol is drawn by Bag's own code. The fonts below ship in the app.

#### Material Icons font (bundled by Flutter 3.47.9)

`fonts/MaterialIcons-Regular.otf` · © Google. Release builds carry a modified copy: Flutter's icon tree shaking keeps only the glyphs Bag uses · Licence: CC-BY-4.0 · https://github.com/google/material-design-icons
